\paragraph{Task 5: Report Assembly - Detailed Workflow}\mbox{}\par

This document provides step-by-step instructions for executing Task 5 (Report Assembly) of the initiating-coverage skill.


\subparagraph{Task Overview}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Purpose}: Write and assemble the comprehensive final DOCX report.


\textbf{Prerequisites}: ⚠ Verify before starting - ALL PREVIOUS TASKS REQUIRED

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Required}: Company research from Task 1
\item {} \textbf{Required}: Financial model from Task 2
\item {} \textbf{Required}: Valuation analysis from Task 3
\item {} \textbf{Required}: Chart files from Task 4
\end{itemize}

\textbf{⚠ CRITICAL: DO NOT START THIS TASK UNLESS ALL TASKS 1-4 ARE COMPLETE}


This is the final assembly task. It cannot be completed without all previous work products.


\textbf{IF ANY OF TASKS 1, 2, 3, OR 4 ARE NOT COMPLETE}: Stop immediately and inform the user which tasks need to be completed first. The specific requirements are:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Task 1: Company research document (6-8K words)
\item {} Task 2: Financial model with all 6 tabs
\item {} Task 3: Valuation analysis with price target and recommendation
\item {} Task 4: Charts zip file with 25-35 charts
\end{itemize}

Do not attempt to create placeholder content, substitute missing sections, or assemble an incomplete report. The report requires ALL inputs to be publication-ready.


\textbf{Output}: Comprehensive Equity Research Report (.docx)

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Length: 30-50 pages (MINIMUM 30)
\item {} Word count: 10,000-15,000 words (MINIMUM 10,000)
\item {} Charts: 25-35 embedded images
\item {} Tables: 12-20 comprehensive tables
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{🔥 CRITICAL INSTRUCTION: SPARE NO TOKENS OR EFFORT}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{THIS IS THE FINAL DELIVERABLE. GO ALL OUT. NO SHORTCUTS. NO ABBREVIATIONS.}


After completing 4 previous tasks, this final task assembles everything into publication-ready institutional research. \textbf{This must be PERFECT.}


\subparagraph{Absolute Requirements}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{DO:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Use ENTIRE token budget if needed} - This is what it's for
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Write EVERY section in FULL} - Not summaries, not placeholders, FULL CONTENT
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Include ALL 25-35 charts} - Embed every single chart from Task 4 throughout the document
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Create ALL 12-20 tables} - Extract every financial table from Excel, don't skip any
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Copy ALL 6-8K words from Task 1} - Use Company 101 content verbatim (40-50\% of report)
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Write 2,000-3,000 words on Projection Assumptions} - Product-by-product, region-by-region detail
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Write 1,500-2,000 words on Scenario Analysis} - Specific Bull/Base/Bear parameters
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Achieve 10,000-15,000 total words} - This is a MINIMUM, not a suggestion
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Produce 30-50 pages minimum} - Text-dense with charts every 200-300 words
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Professional institutional quality} - Indistinguishable from JPMorgan/Goldman Sachs
\end{itemize}

\textbf{NEVER:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ❌ ``This section would include...'' - WRITE THE ACTUAL SECTION
\item {} ❌ ``Charts would be inserted here...'' - INSERT THE ACTUAL CHARTS
\item {} ❌ ``See financial model for details...'' - EXTRACT AND WRITE THE DETAILS
\item {} ❌ ``For brevity, we'll summarize...'' - NO SUMMARIZING, WRITE IN FULL
\item {} ❌ Skip sections to conserve tokens - USE WHATEVER TOKENS ARE NEEDED
\item {} ❌ Create abbreviated versions - EVERY SECTION MUST BE COMPLETE
\item {} ❌ Reference external files instead of including content - INCLUDE EVERYTHING
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Quality Standard}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{This report will be read by institutional investors making million-dollar decisions.}


It must be:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Complete}: Every section written in full with no placeholders
\item {} \textbf{Comprehensive}: All data extracted and included, all charts embedded
\item {} \textbf{Professional}: Proper formatting, citations, tables, charts throughout
\item {} \textbf{Thorough}: Deep analysis with specific numbers, detailed assumptions, complete scenarios
\item {} \textbf{Dense}: 60-80\% page coverage with text and visuals on every page
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Creating the final work product of a 6-10 hour equity research process. Make it count.}


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Critical Sections with Word Counts}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Report Structure}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Page 1: Investment Summary (CRITICAL PAGE)}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{This is the most important page. Must have:}


\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{``INITIATING COVERAGE'' header} (NOT ``Company Update'')
\item {} \textbf{Thesis-focused title} (e.g., ``AI Platform Leader Positioned for 40\% CAGR'')
\item {} \textbf{Rating box} with:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Rating (BUY/OUTPERFORM/HOLD/UNDERPERFORM/SELL)
\item {} Current price
\item {} Target price
\item {} 52-week range
\item {} Market cap
\item {} Enterprise value
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{Research analyst information} with credentials
\item {} \textbf{Stock price performance chart} (Figure 1)
\item {} \textbf{3-4 detailed investment bullets} with ■ character
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Each bullet has \textbf{bold topic header} + 3-5 sentences
\item {} Lead with key numbers
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{Financial summary table} (2-3 years historical + 2-3 years projected)
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Years noted as ``A'' for actual, ``E'' for estimate
\end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Bullet Format Example:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ■ \textbf{Vertical SaaS leadership and regulatory moat should enable \$50bn+ TAM by 2030.}
\item {} Deep domain expertise in healthcare IT, strong customer retention (95\%+ net revenue retention),
\item {} and cross-sell capabilities have driven Acme Health's market expansion. With the healthcare IT
\item {} market expected to reach \$50bn+ by 2030, Acme Health is well-positioned to capture share given
\item {} its regulatory moat and high switching costs. Management has indicated that 70\% of current
\item {} revenue comes from enterprise hospital systems, suggesting strong product-market fit.
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Pages 2-5: Investment Thesis \& Risks}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Investment Thesis (800-1,200 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 3-5 key thesis pillars
\item {} Each pillar: 200-300 words
\item {} Lead with key statistic
\item {} Quantify financial impact
\item {} Include timeline
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Risk Assessment (600-900 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 8-12 identified risks
\item {} Organized by category:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Company-specific risks (4-6)
\item {} Industry/market risks (3-4)
\item {} Financial risks (2-3)
\item {} Macroeconomic risks (2-3)
\end{itemize}
\item {} Each risk: 50-100 word description
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Pages 6-17: Company 101}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Company Description (800-1,200 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} What the company does (plain English)
\item {} Business model and monetization
\item {} Geographic presence
\item {} Scale metrics
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Company History (800-1,200 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Founding story
\item {} Timeline of major milestones
\item {} Strategic pivots
\item {} Recent developments
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Management Team (1,000-1,400 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 300-400 word bio for each of 3-4 key executives
\item {} Include: role, background, accomplishments, education
\item {} Governance structure
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Products \& Services (700-1,000 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Detailed product portfolio
\item {} Features and differentiation
\item {} Target customers
\item {} Pricing models
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Customers \& Go-to-Market (500-700 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Customer segments
\item {} Distribution channels
\item {} Sales strategy
\item {} Key partnerships
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Industry Overview (800-1,200 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Industry definition and scope
\item {} Market size and growth
\item {} Key trends
\item {} Regulatory environment
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Competitive Landscape (700-1,000 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 5-10 key competitors
\item {} Market positioning
\item {} Competitive advantages
\item {} Market share analysis
\end{itemize}

\textbf{TAM Analysis (500-700 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Total addressable market sizing
\item {} Market growth projections
\item {} Company's serviceable market
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Pages 18-30: Financial Analysis}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Historical Financial Analysis (1,200-1,800 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Revenue trends and drivers
\item {} Margin evolution
\item {} Cash flow analysis
\item {} Key metrics trajectory
\item {} Historical context
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Projection Assumptions (2,000-3,000 words)} ⭐ CRITICAL


\textbf{MUST be extremely detailed. Structure:}


\textbf{A. Revenue by Product Assumptions (1,000-1,500 words)}


For EACH major product category:

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} [Product Category A] Revenue Assumptions
\item {} We project [Product A] revenue to grow from \$XXM in 2024A to \$XXM in 2029E,
\item {} representing a XX\% CAGR. This growth is driven by:
\item {} 1. [Driver 1 with specific quantification]
\item {} - Specific metric: from XX to XX
\item {} - Timeline: achieving YY by 2026E
\item {} - Basis: [source or rationale]
\item {} 2. [Driver 2 with specific quantification]
\item {} 3. [Driver 3 with specific quantification]
\item {} [... 8-12 detailed points total for this product ...]
\item {} Specific assumptions by year:
\item {} - 2025E: XX\% growth driven by [specific factors]
\item {} - 2026E: XX\% growth as [specific factors]
\item {} - 2027-2029E: XX\% CAGR as [longer-term factors]
\item {} Key risks to these assumptions include [specific risks].
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Repeat for EACH major product category.}


\textbf{B. Geographic Revenue Assumptions (500-800 words)}


For EACH major region:

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} [Region] Revenue Assumptions
\item {} We project [Region] revenue to grow XX\% CAGR from 2024-2029E, reaching \$XXM, driven by:
\item {} 1. [Market dynamic with quantification]
\item {} 2. [Distribution expansion with specifics]
\item {} 3. [Competitive positioning]
\item {} [... 6-8 detailed points total for this region ...]
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Repeat for EACH major geographic region.}


\textbf{C. Other Key Assumptions (500-700 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Gross margin evolution (with specific drivers and bridge)
\item {} Operating expense assumptions (R\&D, S\&M, G\&A as \% of revenue)
\item {} Working capital assumptions (DSO, DIO, DPO with specific days)
\item {} CapEx as \% of sales (with justification)
\item {} Tax rate assumptions
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Scenario Analysis (1,500-2,000 words)} ⭐ CRITICAL


\textbf{MUST have specific parameters for each scenario. Structure:}


\textbf{Bull Case (500-700 words)}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Bull Case: [Title describing key optimistic scenario]
\item {} Probability: XX\%
\item {} Key Assumptions:
\item {} - Revenue CAGR (2024-2029E): XX\% (vs. XX\% base case)
\item {} - 2029E Revenue: \$X,XXXm (vs. \$X,XXXm base)
\item {} - 2029E EBITDA Margin: XX\% (vs. XX\% base)
\item {} - Key product growth: XX\% CAGR (vs. XX\% base)
\item {} - Geographic expansion: [specific milestones and timeline]
\item {} - Market share: XX\% by 2029E (vs. XX\% base)
\item {} Catalysts Required for Bull Case:
\item {} 1. [Specific catalyst] - Expected timing: [date/quarter]
\item {} 2. [Specific catalyst] - Expected timing: [date/quarter]
\item {} 3. [Specific catalyst] - Expected timing: [date/quarter]
\item {} Detailed Rationale:
\item {} [200-300 words explaining what needs to happen for bull case to materialize.
\item {} Be specific about product launches, market conditions, competitive dynamics, etc.]
\item {} Valuation Implications:
\item {} - DCF Value: \$XX per share (XX\% upside from current)
\item {} - Trading Comps: XX.Xx EV/EBITDA implies \$XX per share
\item {} - Bull Case Target: \$XX per share
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Base Case (300-500 words)}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Base Case: [Title describing most likely scenario]
\item {} Probability: XX\%
\item {} Key Assumptions:
\item {} [Similar structure to Bull Case with base assumptions]
\item {} Rationale:
\item {} [Explain why this is most likely scenario]
\item {} Valuation:
\item {} - DCF Value: \$XX per share
\item {} - Trading Comps: \$XX per share
\item {} - Base Case Target: \$XX per share (weighted average)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Bear Case (500-700 words)}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Bear Case: [Title describing downside scenario]
\item {} Probability: XX\%
\item {} Key Assumptions:
\item {} [Similar structure with downside parameters]
\item {} Downside Triggers:
\item {} 1. [Specific risk event] - Likelihood: [\%]
\item {} 2. [Specific risk event] - Likelihood: [\%]
\item {} 3. [Specific risk event] - Likelihood: [\%]
\item {} Rationale:
\item {} [200-300 words on what would cause bear case]
\item {} Valuation Implications:
\item {} - DCF Value: \$XX per share (XX\% downside from current)
\item {} - Trading Comps: \$XX per share
\item {} - Bear Case Target: \$XX per share
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Scenario Comparison (200-300 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Comprehensive comparison table with key metrics
\item {} Analysis of probability-weighted outcomes
\item {} Risk/reward assessment
\item {} Path dependency discussion
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Growth Drivers (800-1,200 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 3-5 key growth drivers
\item {} Each quantified with specific opportunity size
\item {} Timeline and milestones
\item {} Supporting data from model
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Pages 31-40: Valuation Analysis}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Valuation Methodology (800-1,200 words)}


\textbf{DCF Analysis (300-400 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Methodology explanation
\item {} Key assumptions:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} WACC: X.X\% (calculation breakdown)
\item {} Terminal growth: X.X\% (rationale)
\item {} Terminal margin: XX\% (justification)
\end{itemize}
\item {} Sensitivity analysis discussion
\item {} DCF value: \$XX per share
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Comparable Companies (300-400 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Peer selection rationale (why these 5-10 companies)
\item {} Statistical summary (max/75th/median/25th/min)
\item {} Multiple selection (why EV/EBITDA vs. EV/Revenue vs. P/E)
\item {} Premium/discount justification (why target deserves premium/discount)
\item {} Comparable companies value: \$XX per share
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Precedent Transactions (200-300 words, if applicable)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Transaction relevance
\item {} Control premium analysis
\item {} Precedent transactions value: \$XX per share
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Valuation Reconciliation (200-300 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Weighting rationale (e.g., DCF 50\%, Comps 40\%, Precedent 10\%)
\item {} Weighted average calculation
\item {} Valuation range (low/base/high)
\item {} Final price target: \$XX
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Price Target \& Recommendation (300-500 words)}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Final recommendation (BUY/OUTPERFORM/HOLD/UNDERPERFORM/SELL)
\item {} Price target: \$XX (XX\% upside from current \$XX)
\item {} Time horizon: 12 months
\item {} Key catalysts (3-5 with specific timeframes)
\item {} Key risks to price target (3-5 with impact quantification)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Pages 41-50: Appendices}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Data Sources \& References}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} All sources listed with dates
\item {} Organized by category:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} SEC Filings (with EDGAR links)
\item {} Earnings Calls (with transcript links)
\item {} Company Materials
\item {} Industry Reports
\item {} News Articles
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{ALL URLs must be clickable hyperlinks}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Detailed Financial Model Assumptions}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Comprehensive assumptions detail
\item {} Calculation methodologies
\item {} Data sources for historical figures
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Additional Supporting Tables}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Extended financial projections
\item {} Detailed comparable companies data
\item {} Sensitivity analyses
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Report Assembly Philosophy}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{CRITICAL PRINCIPLE 1}: A good equity research report is \textbf{text-dense with lots of illustrating images}.


\textbf{Target density}: 60-80\% page coverage

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Every page should have BOTH text AND visuals
\item {} Charts should be interspersed throughout text, not grouped
\item {} Average 1 chart per page minimum (30-50 pages = 25-35+ charts)
\item {} Tables should break up large text blocks
\end{itemize}

\textbf{CRITICAL PRINCIPLE 2}: Use Claude's DOCX and XLSX skills to programmatically create the report.


\textbf{REQUIRED TOOLS} (Claude has built-in skills for these):

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{DOCX skill} - To create and manipulate Word documents
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Read Task 1 .md file → Convert to Word formatting
\item {} Insert images from Task 4 chart files
\item {} Create tables
\item {} Format text, headers, footers, page numbers
\item {} Add hyperlinks
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{XLSX skill} - To read data from Excel files
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Extract tables from Task 2 financial model
\item {} Read Task 3 valuation tabs
\item {} Pull historical financials from Task 1
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{Direct file operations} - Work with actual files
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Read: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Research\_\allowbreak{}Document\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md}
\item {} Read: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx}
\item {} Read: \emph{chart\_\allowbreak{}01.png}, \emph{chart\_\allowbreak{}02.png}, etc.
\item {} Write: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Initiation\_\allowbreak{}Report\_\allowbreak{}[Date].docx}
\end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{DO NOT}: Manually copy/paste or describe what should be done \textbf{DO}: Use Claude's DOCX and XLSX skills to open files, extract data, and create the DOCX report


\textbf{Content Reuse Strategy}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Task 1 content (40-50\% of report)}: Read .md file → Convert to Word format → Add charts
\item {} \textbf{Task 2/3 data (30-40\% of report)}: Read .xlsx file → Extract tables → Write interpretation
\item {} \textbf{Original writing (10-20\% of report)}: Investment thesis, projection assumptions, scenario analysis
\end{itemize}

\textbf{This approach}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Maximizes efficiency (no rewriting 6-8K words that are already good)
\item {} Maintains quality (Task 1 content is substantive, professional analysis)
\item {} Focuses effort on value-add (quantitative interpretation and investment thesis)
\item {} Uses actual files programmatically (not manual work)
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Step-by-Step Report Assembly Workflow}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Step 1: Organize All Inputs and Verify Files}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Verify all input files exist:}


Use Claude's file operations to check:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Research\_\allowbreak{}Document\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md} (Task 1)
\item {} \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Historical\_\allowbreak{}Financials\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx} (Task 1)
\item {} \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx} (Task 2 with Task 3 tabs)
\item {} \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Valuation\_\allowbreak{}Analysis\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md} (Task 3)
\item {} \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Charts\_\allowbreak{}[Date].zip} (Task 4) - \textbf{Extract this first}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Step 1a: Extract Charts from Zip File}


Before proceeding, extract all chart files from the Task 4 zip:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Locate \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Charts\_\allowbreak{}[Date].zip}
\item {} Extract all contents to a working directory (e.g., \emph{task4\_\allowbreak{}charts/})
\item {} Verify 25-35 PNG files were extracted
\item {} Verify chart\_\allowbreak{}index.txt is present
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Expected folder structure after extraction:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Report\_\allowbreak{}Working/
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Research\_\allowbreak{}Document\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Historical\_\allowbreak{}Financials\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx (includes Task 3 valuation tabs)
\item {} [Task 2 tabs: Revenue Model, Income Statement, Scenarios, etc.]
\item {} [Task 3 tabs: DCF, Sensitivity, Comps, Valuation Summary]
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Valuation\_\allowbreak{}Analysis\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Charts\_\allowbreak{}[Date].zip

\subparagraph{Step 3: Write Quantitative Sections}\mbox{}\par

These sections interpret the financial model.


\textbf{Write in this order:}


\subparagraph{A. Financial Analysis (1,200-1,800 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Analyze historical performance from model
\item {} Discuss trends in revenue, margins, cash flow
\item {} Reference specific charts and tables
\item {} Lead with numbers
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{B. Projection Assumptions (2,000-3,000 words) ⭐ CRITICAL}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Follow detailed structure from Report Structure section above
\item {} Must be product-by-product (8-12 points per product)
\item {} Must be region-by-region (6-8 points per region)
\item {} Must include margin, opex, capex, working capital assumptions
\item {} \textbf{This section separates amateur from professional analysis}
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{C. Scenario Analysis (1,500-2,000 words) ⭐ CRITICAL}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Follow detailed structure from Report Structure section above
\item {} Bull case: specific parameters, catalysts, probability, valuation
\item {} Base case: most likely scenario with rationale
\item {} Bear case: downside triggers and parameters
\item {} Comparison table and analysis
\item {} \textbf{Must have specific quantified parameters for each scenario}
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{D. Growth Drivers (800-1,200 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 3-5 key drivers with quantified opportunities
\item {} Timeline and milestones
\item {} Evidence from model
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{E. Valuation Methodology (800-1,200 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} DCF explanation with assumptions
\item {} Comparables rationale
\item {} Precedent transactions (if applicable)
\item {} Reconciliation and weighting
\item {} Price target derivation
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Step 4: Write Synthesis Sections}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Write in this order:}


\subparagraph{A. Investment Thesis (800-1,200 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 3-5 key pillars
\item {} Each pillar: 200-300 words
\item {} Lead with key statistic
\item {} Quantify financial impact
\item {} Include timeline
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{B. Risk Assessment (600-900 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Pull from Task 1 research document
\item {} Organize into 4 categories
\item {} 8-12 risks total
\item {} Each risk: 50-100 words
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{C. Price Target \& Recommendation (300-500 words)}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Final recommendation
\item {} Price target with upside \%
\item {} Key catalysts with timeframes
\item {} Key risks to target
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{D. Investment Summary (500-700 words) - WRITE LAST}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Page 1 content
\item {} 3-4 detailed bullets with bold headers
\item {} Complete synthesis of all findings
\item {} \textbf{Write this section LAST after full analysis complete}
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Writing Style Guidelines}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Lead with Numbers (CRITICAL)}\mbox{}\par

✓ \textbf{CORRECT}: ``Revenue increased 150\% YoY to \$250M in Q4 2024, driven by...'' ✗ \textbf{INCORRECT}: ``The company saw strong revenue growth this quarter...''


✓ \textbf{CORRECT}: ``EBITDA margin expanded 500bps to 30\% vs. 25\% in FY2023'' ✗ \textbf{INCORRECT}: ``EBITDA margin expanded versus the prior year''


✓ \textbf{CORRECT}: ``Market share increased 3 percentage points to 18\% vs. 15\% in 2023'' ✗ \textbf{INCORRECT}: ``Market share increased compared to last year''


✓ \textbf{CORRECT}: ``Management expects 40-50\% revenue growth in FY2025E'' ✗ \textbf{INCORRECT}: ``Management expects strong revenue growth''


\subparagraph{Professional Writing Standards}\mbox{}\par

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Front-load}: Most important information first
\item {} \textbf{Data-driven}: Lead with numbers and metrics
\item {} \textbf{Specific}: Concrete examples, not generic statements
\item {} \textbf{Objective}: Present facts, acknowledge risks
\item {} \textbf{Confident}: State views clearly with supporting evidence
\item {} \textbf{Active voice}: ``We estimate revenue will reach \$500M''
\item {} \textbf{Precise}: Avoid ``might'', ``could'', ``possibly''
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Number Formatting}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Consistency:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Billions: \$X.XB (e.g., ``\$2.5B'')
\item {} Millions: \$XXXM (e.g., ``\$250M'')
\item {} Always specify: YoY, QoQ, CAGR
\item {} Basis points for small margin changes: ``500bps''
\item {} Year format: ``2024A'' (actual), ``2025E'' (estimate)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Use ``vs.'' not ``versus''}\mbox{}\par
✓ \textbf{CORRECT}: ``Gross margin of 65\% vs. 60\% in prior year'' ✗ \textbf{INCORRECT}: ``Gross margin of 65\% versus 60\%''


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Common Pitfalls to Avoid}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{⚠ MOST COMMON MISTAKE: TAKING SHORTCUTS DUE TO LENGTH}


Many reports fail because they use placeholders like ``details would be included here'' or ``see model for data'' instead of actually writing/extracting the content. \textbf{DO NOT DO THIS.} Write every section in full. Extract every table. Embed every chart. Use whatever tokens are needed.


\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Rewriting Task 1 content}: DO NOT rewrite the 6-8K words from Task 1. Use almost verbatim - just reformat and add charts. Focus writing effort on quantitative sections (projections, scenarios, valuation).
\item {} \textbf{Sparse pages}: Every page must have BOTH text AND visuals. Target 60-80\% page density. Insert charts every 200-300 words.
\item {} \textbf{Grouping charts at end}: Charts must be interspersed throughout text, not grouped. Place chart immediately after paragraph discussing that topic.
\item {} \textbf{Writing in markdown}: Use DOCX format, NOT markdown
\item {} \textbf{Skipping Page 1 format}: Must follow exact institutional format
\item {} \textbf{Generic bullets}: Page 1 bullets need bold headers + specific data
\item {} \textbf{Short sections}: Must meet minimum word counts
\item {} \textbf{Thin assumptions}: Projection Assumptions MUST be 2,000-3,000 words with product-by-product and region-by-region detail
\item {} \textbf{Vague scenarios}: Must have specific parameters for Bull/Base/Bear
\item {} \textbf{Plain text URLs}: All citations must be clickable hyperlinks
\item {} \textbf{Missing statistical summary}: Comps table must have max/75th/median/25th/min
\item {} \textbf{Charts not embedded}: All 25-35 charts must be IN document, not just referenced
\item {} \textbf{Numbers don't match model}: Verify all figures against source
\item {} \textbf{Skipping verification}: Quality check is NOT optional
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subsection{Skill Resource: skills/initiating-coverage/references/valuation-methodologies.md}\label{file:skills-initiating-coverage-references-valuation-methodologies-md}

\paragraph{Valuation Methodologies for Equity Research}\mbox{}\par

This reference document provides comprehensive guidance on the three primary valuation methodologies used in equity research: Discounted Cash Flow (DCF), Trading Comparables, and Precedent Transactions.


\subparagraph{Table of Contents}\mbox{}\par

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \href{\#discounted-cash-flow-dcf-analysis}{Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Analysis}
\item {} \href{\#trading-comparables-analysis}{Trading Comparables Analysis}
\item {} \href{\#precedent-transactions-analysis}{Precedent Transactions Analysis}
\item {} \href{\#valuation-reconciliation}{Valuation Reconciliation}
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Discounted Cash Flow (DCF) Analysis}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Overview}\mbox{}\par

DCF analysis values a company based on the present value of its projected future cash flows. This is considered the most theoretically sound valuation method as it's based on fundamental value creation.


\subparagraph{Step-by-Step DCF Process}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{1. Historical Financial Analysis}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Collect 3-5 years of historical financials
\item {} Calculate historical FCF = EBIT(1-Tax Rate) + D\&A - CapEx - Change in NWC
\item {} Analyze historical growth rates and margins
\item {} Identify trends and cyclicality
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{2. Build Revenue Projections (5-10 years)}\mbox{}\par
\textbf{Approaches:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Top-down}: Start with market size (TAM) → Market share → Revenue
\item {} \textbf{Bottom-up}: Units sold × Price per unit
\item {} \textbf{Hybrid}: Combine multiple drivers
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Key Considerations:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Management guidance and historical growth
\item {} Industry growth rates and market trends
\item {} Competitive dynamics and market share evolution
\item {} Product pipeline and new market opportunities
\item {} Macroeconomic factors
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{3. Project Operating Expenses}\mbox{}\par
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{COGS}: As \% of revenue (analyze historical margins)
\item {} \textbf{SG\&A}: Often semi-fixed; model as \% of revenue with scale effects
\item {} \textbf{R\&D}: Critical for tech/pharma; model as \% of revenue
\item {} \textbf{D\&A}: Based on CapEx assumptions
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Calculate EBIT} = Revenue - COGS - Operating Expenses


\subparagraph{4. Calculate Unlevered Free Cash Flow}\mbox{}\par
\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} EBIT
\item {} × (1 - Tax Rate)
\item {} = NOPAT (Net Operating Profit After Tax)
\item {} + Depreciation \& Amortization
\item {} - Capital Expenditures
\item {} - Increase in Net Working Capital
\item {} = Unlevered Free Cash Flow (UFCF)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{CapEx Assumptions:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Maintenance CapEx: Required to maintain current operations (typically 2-4\% of revenue)
\item {} Growth CapEx: Required for expansion
\item {} Consider industry benchmarks and company guidance
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Net Working Capital:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} NWC = (Accounts Receivable + Inventory) - Accounts Payable
\item {} Model as \% of revenue or days (DSO, DIO, DPO)
\item {} An increase in NWC is a use of cash
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{5. Determine Terminal Value}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Method A: Perpetuity Growth Method}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Terminal Value = FCF(final year) × (1 + g) / (WACC - g)
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} g = perpetual growth rate (typically 2-3\%, not exceeding GDP growth)
\item {} Use when company has reached stable, mature growth
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Method B: Exit Multiple Method}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Terminal Value = EBITDA(final year) × Exit Multiple
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Exit multiple based on current trading comps
\item {} More appropriate for cyclical businesses
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{6. Calculate Weighted Average Cost of Capital (WACC)}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} WACC = (E/V × Cost of Equity) + (D/V × Cost of Debt × (1 - Tax Rate))
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Cost of Equity (using CAPM):}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Cost of Equity = Risk-Free Rate + Beta × Equity Risk Premium
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Risk-Free Rate: 10-year Treasury yield
\item {} Beta: Regression of stock returns vs. market (or use comparable beta)
\item {} Equity Risk Premium: Historical average \textasciitilde{}5-6\%
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Cost of Debt:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Cost of Debt = Risk-Free Rate + Credit Spread
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Use company's current borrowing rate or implied from bonds
\item {} Adjust for credit rating if no bonds outstanding
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Capital Structure:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} E/V = Market value of equity / Total value
\item {} D/V = Market value of debt / Total value
\item {} Use target capital structure, not current (if significantly different)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{7. Discount Cash Flows to Present Value}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} PV = Σ [FCFt / (1 + WACC)\textasciicircum{}t] + [Terminal Value / (1 + WACC)\textasciicircum{}n]
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{8. Calculate Enterprise Value and Equity Value}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Enterprise Value = PV of Projected FCF + PV of Terminal Value
\item {} Less: Net Debt (Total Debt - Cash)
\item {} Plus: Non-operating Assets
\item {} Less: Minority Interest
\item {} Less: Preferred Stock
\item {} = Equity Value
\item {} Price Per Share = Equity Value / Diluted Shares Outstanding
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{DCF Sensitivity Analysis}\mbox{}\par

Always perform sensitivity analysis on key variables:


\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Two-way sensitivity table}: WACC vs. Terminal Growth Rate
\item {} \textbf{Revenue growth scenarios}: Base / Bull / Bear cases
\item {} \textbf{Margin assumptions}: Operating leverage scenarios
\item {} \textbf{Terminal multiple sensitivity}: If using exit multiple method
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Example Sensitivity Table:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Terminal Growth Rate
\item {} WACC 2.0\% 2.5\% 3.0\%
\item {} 8.0\% \$45 \$48 \$52
\item {} 9.0\% \$40 \$43 \$46
\item {} 10.0\% \$36 \$39 \$41
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Common DCF Pitfalls to Avoid}\mbox{}\par

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Double-counting growth}: Don't project high growth without corresponding investment (CapEx, NWC)
\item {} \textbf{Unrealistic terminal growth}: Should not exceed long-term GDP growth
\item {} \textbf{Ignoring cyclicality}: Normalize earnings for cyclical businesses
\item {} \textbf{Wrong cash flow definition}: Use unlevered FCF, not net income
\item {} \textbf{Inconsistent assumptions}: Match discount rate to cash flows (unlevered FCF → WACC)
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Trading Comparables Analysis}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Overview}\mbox{}\par

Trading comps values a company based on how similar companies are valued in the public markets. This reflects current market sentiment and relative valuation.


\subparagraph{Step-by-Step Comps Process}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{1. Select Comparable Companies}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Selection Criteria:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Same industry/sector (primary criterion)
\item {} Similar business model and revenue streams
\item {} Comparable size (market cap, revenue)
\item {} Similar growth profile and margins
\item {} Similar end markets and geographies
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Typical Universe:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Start with 8-15 companies
\item {} Remove companies with unique circumstances
\item {} Final set of 5-10 companies
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{2. Gather Financial Information}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Required Data:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Current stock price and shares outstanding
\item {} Latest fiscal year financial statements
\item {} Next-year (NTM) estimates from consensus
\item {} Historical growth rates
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Calculate Market Metrics:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Market Cap = Share Price × Shares Outstanding
\item {} Enterprise Value = Market Cap + Debt + Minority Interest + Preferred - Cash
\item {} Net Debt = Total Debt - Cash \& Equivalents
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{3. Calculate Valuation Multiples}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Enterprise Value Multiples:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{EV/Revenue}: Good for early-stage/high-growth companies
\item {} \textbf{EV/EBITDA}: Most common; good for capital-intensive businesses
\item {} \textbf{EV/EBIT}: Useful when D\&A varies significantly
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Equity Value Multiples:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{P/E (Price/Earnings)}: Most widely used
\item {} \textbf{P/B (Price/Book)}: Good for financial institutions
\item {} \textbf{P/S (Price/Sales)}: For unprofitable companies
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Calculate for:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Last Twelve Months (LTM) - historical
\item {} Next Twelve Months (NTM) - forward-looking (preferred)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{4. Analyze and Select Multiples}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Create Comparable Company Table:}


\begingroup
\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
\begin{longtable}{>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}}
\toprule
{} \textbf{Company} & \textbf{Market Cap} & \textbf{EV/ Revenue} & \textbf{EV/ EBITDA} & \textbf{EV/ EBIT} & \textbf{P/ E (NTM)} & \textbf{Revenue Growth} & \textbf{EBITDA Margin} \\
\midrule
{} Comp A & \$10B & 3.5x & 12.0x & 18.0x & 22.0x & 15\% & 28\% \\
{} Comp B & \$8B & 3.0x & 10.5x & 16.0x & 19.0x & 12\% & 27\% \\
{} ... & ... & ... & ... & ... & ... & ... & ... \\
{} Median & - & \textbf{3.2x} & \textbf{11.0x} & \textbf{17.0x} & \textbf{20.5x} & 13\% & 27.5\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\textbf{Adjustments:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Remove outliers (typically >2 standard deviations)
\item {} Consider using median instead of mean (less affected by outliers)
\item {} Weight multiples if some comps are more comparable
\item {} Adjust for differences in growth, margins, risk
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{5. Apply Multiples to Target Company}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Example Calculation:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Target Company NTM EBITDA = \$500M
\item {} Selected EV/EBITDA Multiple = 11.0x
\item {} Implied Enterprise Value = \$500M × 11.0x = \$5,500M
\item {} Less: Net Debt = \$1,000M
\item {} Equity Value = \$4,500M
\item {} Shares Outstanding = 100M
\item {} Implied Price Per Share = \$45.00
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{6. Select Appropriate Multiple}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Choose based on:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{EV/Revenue}: High-growth, unprofitable companies (tech, biotech pre-profit)
\item {} \textbf{EV/EBITDA}: Most common; capital-intensive industries (manufacturing, telecom)
\item {} \textbf{P/E}: Profitable companies with stable cap structure (consumer, retail)
\item {} \textbf{Sector-specific}: P/B for banks, EV/Production for oil \& gas, EV/Subscriber for media
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Premium/Discount Analysis}\mbox{}\par

Apply premiums or discounts based on:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Growth premium}: Higher growth → higher multiple
\item {} \textbf{Profitability}: Higher margins → higher multiple
\item {} \textbf{Size}: Larger companies typically trade at premium (liquidity)
\item {} \textbf{Market position}: Market leaders → premium
\item {} \textbf{Geographic}: Developed vs. emerging markets
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Precedent Transactions Analysis}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Overview}\mbox{}\par

Precedent transactions values a company based on prices paid for similar companies in M\&A transactions. This reflects control premiums and strategic value.


\subparagraph{Step-by-Step Process}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{1. Identify Relevant Transactions}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Selection Criteria:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Same or similar industry
\item {} Similar size (within 0.5x to 2x target's size)
\item {} Similar business characteristics
\item {} Recent transactions (last 3-5 years preferred)
\item {} Announced and closed deals (avoid withdrawn deals)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Typical Universe:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 5-10 transactions minimum
\item {} Focus on recent deals (weight more recent higher)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{2. Gather Transaction Details}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Required Information:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Transaction date (announcement and close)
\item {} Acquisition price and structure (cash, stock, mixed)
\item {} Target's financials at time of transaction
\item {} Strategic rationale and synergies
\item {} Control premium paid
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Sources:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} SEC filings (S-4, 8-K, proxy statements)
\item {} Press releases and investor presentations
\item {} M\&A databases (CapIQ, FactSet, Bloomberg)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{3. Calculate Transaction Multiples}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Same multiples as trading comps, but based on transaction value:}


\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Transaction Value = Equity Purchase Price + Assumed Debt - Cash Acquired
\item {} EV/Revenue (LTM) = Transaction Value / Target's LTM Revenue
\item {} EV/EBITDA (LTM) = Transaction Value / Target's LTM EBITDA
\item {} EV/EBIT (LTM) = Transaction Value / Target's LTM EBIT
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Calculate Control Premium:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Control Premium = (Offer Price - Unaffected Price) / Unaffected Price
\end{itemize}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Unaffected Price = Target's stock price 1-2 days before announcement
\item {} Typical range: 20-40\%
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{4. Analyze Precedent Transactions}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Create Precedent Transactions Table:}


\begingroup
\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
\begin{longtable}{>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.115\textwidth}}
\toprule
{} \textbf{Date} & \textbf{Target} & \textbf{Acquirer} & \textbf{Deal Value} & \textbf{EV/ Revenue} & \textbf{EV/ EBITDA} & \textbf{Premium} & \textbf{Rationale} \\
\midrule
{} Q1'24 & CompX & BuyerA & \$5.0B & 4.0x & 14.0x & 35\% & Market consolidation \\
{} Q3'23 & CompY & BuyerB & \$3.5B & 3.5x & 12.5x & 28\% & Strategic fit \\
{} Median & - & - & - & \textbf{3.8x} & \textbf{13.0x} & \textbf{31\%} & - \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\subparagraph{5. Apply to Target Company}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Important Considerations:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Precedent multiples typically higher than trading comps (include control premium)
\item {} Adjust for differences in transaction rationale
\item {} Consider market conditions at time of transactions vs. current
\item {} Weight recent transactions more heavily
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Example Calculation:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Target Company LTM EBITDA = \$450M
\item {} Selected EV/EBITDA Multiple = 13.0x (precedent)
\item {} vs Trading Comps Multiple = 11.0x
\item {} Implied EV (Precedent) = \$450M × 13.0x = \$5,850M
\item {} Implied EV (Trading) = \$450M × 11.0x = \$4,950M
\item {} Implied Control Premium = \$5,850M / \$4,950M - 1 = 18\%
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Adjustments to Transaction Multiples}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Consider adjusting for:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Market conditions}: Bull vs. bear market (M\&A activity levels)
\item {} \textbf{Deal structure}: Strategic vs. financial buyer
\item {} \textbf{Synergies}: Transactions with high synergies command premiums
\item {} \textbf{Competitive dynamics}: Single vs. multiple bidders
\item {} \textbf{Time value}: Older transactions less relevant
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Valuation Reconciliation}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Creating a Valuation Bridge}\mbox{}\par

Present all three methods in a single framework:


\textbf{Example Valuation Summary:}


\begingroup
\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
\begin{longtable}{>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.153\textwidth}}
\toprule
{} \textbf{Method} & \textbf{Enterprise Value} & \textbf{Equity Value} & \textbf{Price/ Share} & \textbf{Weight} & \textbf{Implied Value} \\
\midrule
{} DCF Analysis & \$5,200M & \$4,200M & \$42.00 & 50\% & \$21.00 \\
{} Trading Comps & \$5,500M & \$4,500M & \$45.00 & 30\% & \$13.50 \\
{} Precedent Trans. & \$5,850M & \$4,850M & \$48.50 & 20\% & \$9.70 \\
{} \textbf{Weighted Avg} & - & - & \textbf{\$44.20} & - & \textbf{\$44.20} \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\subparagraph{Weighting the Methods}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Typical Weighting:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{DCF}: 40-60\% (fundamental value, but assumes accuracy of projections)
\item {} \textbf{Trading Comps}: 25-40\% (reflects current market sentiment)
\item {} \textbf{Precedent Trans.}: 15-25\% (less relevant unless M\&A likely)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Adjust weights based on:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Confidence in forecasts}: Higher confidence → higher DCF weight
\item {} \textbf{Market conditions}: Bull/bear market affects comps reliability
\item {} \textbf{M\&A likelihood}: Higher if company in play or industry consolidating
\item {} \textbf{Company maturity}: Mature companies → higher weight on comps; growth → higher DCF weight
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Valuation Range}\mbox{}\par

Always present a valuation range, not a point estimate:


\textbf{Approach:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Base Case}: Most likely scenario
\item {} \textbf{Bull Case}: Optimistic assumptions (revenue growth, margins)
\item {} \textbf{Bear Case}: Conservative assumptions
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Example:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Bear Case: \$38 - \$40
\item {} Base Case: \$42 - \$46
\item {} Bull Case: \$48 - \$52
\item {} Recommendation: BUY with target price of \$45 (midpoint of base case)
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Sanity Checks}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Cross-check valuation with:}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Historical multiples}: Is current valuation in line with history?
\item {} \textbf{Peer comparison}: Justified premium/discount vs. peers?
\item {} \textbf{Implied growth}: What growth is market pricing in?
\item {} \textbf{Implied returns}: IRR from current price to target price
\item {} \textbf{Market cap analysis}: Does total market cap make sense?
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Conclusion}\mbox{}\par

Using all three valuation methods provides a robust framework for determining fair value:


\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{DCF} provides intrinsic value based on fundamentals
\item {} \textbf{Trading Comps} reflects current market valuation
\item {} \textbf{Precedent Transactions} indicates M\&A value and control premium
\end{itemize}

The key is to understand the assumptions driving each method and to present a well-reasoned valuation range that considers multiple scenarios and methodologies.


\vspace{0.8em}\hrule\vspace{0.8em}

\paragraph{Initiating Coverage}\mbox{}\par

Create institutional-quality equity research initiation reports through a structured 5-task workflow. Each task must be executed separately with verified inputs.


\subparagraph{Overview}\mbox{}\par

This skill produces comprehensive first-time coverage reports following institutional standards (JPMorgan, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley format). Tasks are executed individually, each verifying prerequisites before proceeding.


\textbf{Default Font}: Times New Roman throughout all documents (unless user specifies otherwise).


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{⚠ CRITICAL: One Task at a Time}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{THIS SKILL OPERATES IN SINGLE-TASK MODE ONLY.}


\subparagraph{If User Requests Full Pipeline}\mbox{}\par

When user requests:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ``Create a coverage initiation report for [Company]''
\item {} ``Write an initiation report for [Company]''
\item {} ``Do the entire equity research process for [Company]''
\item {} ``Complete all 5 tasks for [Company]''
\item {} Any request that implies running multiple tasks or the entire workflow
\end{itemize}

\textbf{REQUIRED RESPONSE:}


\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Ask which specific task to perform:} ``` I can help you create an equity research initiation report for [Company]. This involves 5 separate tasks that need to be completed individually:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Company Research - Research business, management, industry
\item {} Financial Modeling - Build projection model
\item {} Valuation Analysis - DCF and comparable companies
\item {} Chart Generation - Create 25-35 charts
\item {} Report Assembly - Compile final report Which task would you like to start with? ```
\end{enumerate}
\item {} \textbf{When user explicitly requests all tasks together:} ``\emph{ I understand you'd like to complete the entire initiation report pipeline. Currently, this skill supports executing one task at a time, which allows for better quality control and review at each stage. We're working on a seamless end-to-end workflow that will make this process more automated, but for now, we'll need to complete each task separately. Would you like to start with Task 1 (Company Research)? }``
\item {} \textbf{Never automatically assume which task to start} - always ask user to confirm.
\item {} \textbf{Never execute multiple tasks in sequence} - complete one task, deliver outputs, then wait for next user request.
\end{enumerate}

\subparagraph{Task Execution Rules}\mbox{}\par

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ✅ Execute exactly ONE task per user request
\item {} ✅ Always verify prerequisites before starting a task
\item {} ✅ Deliver task outputs and confirm completion
\item {} ✅ Wait for user to explicitly request the next task
\item {} ❌ Never chain multiple tasks together automatically
\item {} ❌ Never assume user wants to proceed to next task
\item {} ❌ Never execute Tasks 3-5 without verifying required inputs exist
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{⚠ Deliverables Policy: NO SHORTCUTS}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{DELIVER ONLY THE SPECIFIED OUTPUTS. DO NOT CREATE EXTRA DOCUMENTS.}


Each task specifies exact deliverables. Do NOT create:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ❌ ``Completion summaries''
\item {} ❌ ``Executive summaries''
\item {} ❌ ``Quick reference guides''
\item {} ❌ ``Next steps documents''
\item {} ❌ ``Task completion reports''
\item {} ❌ Any other ``helpful'' documentation not explicitly specified
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Why}: These extras waste context and are not part of the professional workflow.


\textbf{What TO deliver}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ✅ Task 1: Research document (.md) — \textbf{NOTHING ELSE}
\item {} ✅ Task 2: Financial model (.xlsx) — \textbf{NOTHING ELSE}
\item {} ✅ Task 3: Valuation analysis (.md) + Excel tabs added to Task 2 file — \textbf{NOTHING ELSE}
\item {} ✅ Task 4: Charts zip file (.zip) — \textbf{NOTHING ELSE}
\item {} ✅ Task 5: Final report (.docx) — \textbf{NOTHING ELSE}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{If a deliverable is not listed above, DO NOT CREATE IT.}


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Task Selection}\mbox{}\par

Select which task to execute:

\subparagraph{How to Use This Skill}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{User Request Patterns and Responses}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Pattern 1: User specifies a specific task}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} User: ``Use initiating-coverage, Task 1 for Tesla''
\item {} Response: ✅ Execute Task 1 immediately
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Pattern 2: User asks for ``initiation report'' or ``full pipeline''}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} User: ``Create a coverage initiation report for Tesla''
\item {} Response: ❌ DO NOT start any task automatically
\item {} ✅ Ask which task to start with (see template above)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Pattern 3: User wants to do ``all tasks'' or ``entire workflow''}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} User: ``I want to complete all 5 tasks for Tesla''
\item {} Response: ❌ DO NOT chain tasks together
\item {} ✅ Explain one-at-a-time limitation (see template above)
\item {} ✅ Ask if they want to start with Task 1
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Correct Usage Examples}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Executing a single task:}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ``Use initiating-coverage skill, Task 1 for Tesla''
\item {} ``Do Task 2 of initiating-coverage for Tesla''
\item {} ``Run Task 3 for Tesla using the initiating-coverage skill''
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Completing full report (requires 5 separate requests):}

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Request 1: ``Do Task 1 for Tesla'' → Complete → Deliver outputs
\item {} Request 2: ``Do Task 2 for Tesla'' → Complete → Deliver outputs
\item {} Request 3: ``Do Task 3 for Tesla'' → Complete → Deliver outputs
\item {} Request 4: ``Do Task 4 for Tesla'' → Complete → Deliver outputs
\item {} Request 5: ``Do Task 5 for Tesla'' → Complete → Deliver outputs
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Task Execution Order}\mbox{}\par

For a complete initiation report, tasks must be executed in separate user requests following this order:


\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Request 1: Task 1 - Company Research (independent)
\item {} ↓ [User reviews outputs and requests next task]
\item {} Request 2: Task 2 - Financial Modeling (independent)
\item {} ↓ [User reviews outputs and requests next task]
\item {} Request 3: Task 3 - Valuation Analysis (requires Task 2 output)
\item {} ↓ [User reviews outputs and requests next task]
\item {} Request 4: Task 4 - Chart Generation (requires Tasks 2 \& 3 outputs)
\item {} ↓ [User reviews outputs and requests next task]
\item {} Request 5: Task 5 - Report Assembly (requires ALL previous task outputs)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Note}: Tasks 1 and 2 can be run in any order. Tasks 3-5 have strict dependencies and must verify inputs before proceeding.


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Task 1: Company Research}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Purpose}: Research company's business, management, competitive position, industry, and risks.


\textbf{Prerequisites}: ✅ None (fully independent)

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Company name or ticker symbol
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Process}:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Verify company name/ticker provided
\item {} Load detailed instructions from references/task1-company-research.md
\item {} Execute qualitative research workflow
\item {} Deliver research document
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Output}: Company Research Document (6,000-8,000 words)

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Company overview \& history
\item {} Management bios (300-400 words × 3-4 execs)
\item {} Products \& services analysis
\item {} Industry overview
\item {} Competitive analysis (5-10 competitors)
\item {} TAM sizing
\item {} Risk assessment (8-12 risks)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{File name}: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Research\_\allowbreak{}Document\_\allowbreak{}[Date].md}


\textbf{⚠ DELIVER ONLY THIS 1 FILE. NO completion summaries, no extra documents.}


\textbf{⚠ DO NOT TAKE SHORTCUTS:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ✅ Write full 6,000-8,000 words (not summaries)
\item {} ✅ Complete 300-400 word bios for ALL 3-4 executives
\item {} ✅ Analyze ALL 5-10 competitors thoroughly
\item {} ✅ Cover all 8-12 risks across 4 categories
\item {} ❌ Do not abbreviate sections to save time
\item {} ❌ Do not skip any required sections
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Verification before proceeding}: None required for this task.


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Task 2: Financial Modeling}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Purpose}: Extract historical financials and build comprehensive Excel financial model with projections and scenarios.


\textbf{Prerequisites}: ⚠ Verify before starting

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Required}: Access to company financial data
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} For public companies: Latest 10-K from SEC EDGAR
\item {} For private companies: Financial statements or available estimates
\item {} OR: Pre-extracted historical financials provided by user
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{Optional}: Company research (Task 1) for business context
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Input Verification}:

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} BEFORE STARTING - Select approach:
\item {} Option A: Extract financials (most common)
\item {} - [ ] Have access to 10-K or financial statements?
\item {} - [ ] Ready to extract 3-5 years of data?
\item {} Option B: User provided pre-extracted financials
\item {} - [ ] Historical financials file received?
\item {} - [ ] Contains income statement, cash flow, balance sheet (3-5 years)?
\item {} Optional:
\item {} - [ ] Company research (Task 1) complete for context?
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Process}:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Verify access to financial data
\item {} Load detailed instructions from references/task2-financial-modeling.md
\item {} \textbf{Step 1}: Extract historical financials (if needed)
\item {} \textbf{Step 2+}: Build projection model with 6 essential tabs
\item {} Deliver Excel model
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Output}: Excel Financial Model (.xlsx)

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} 6 essential tabs:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Revenue Model} - Product breakdown (20-30 rows) + Geography breakdown (15-20 rows)
\item {} \textbf{Income Statement} - Full P\&L with 40-50 line items, historical (3-5 years) + projected (5 years)
\item {} \textbf{Cash Flow Statement} - Operating/Investing/Financing activities, historical + projected
\item {} \textbf{Balance Sheet} - Assets/Liabilities/Equity, historical + projected
\item {} \textbf{Scenarios} - Bull/Base/Bear comparison table
\item {} \textbf{DCF Inputs} - Prepared for Task 3 valuation
\end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{File name}: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx}


\textbf{⚠ DELIVER ONLY THIS 1 FILE. NO completion summaries, no extra documents.}


\textbf{⚠ DO NOT TAKE SHORTCUTS:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ✅ If extracting financials: Extract ALL line items from 3 financial statements (3-5 years)
\item {} ✅ Build ALL 6 projection tabs completely with full detail
\item {} ✅ Create detailed revenue model with 20-30 product rows AND 15-20 geography rows
\item {} ✅ Build complete income statement with 40-50 line items (not abbreviated)
\item {} ✅ Include full cash flow statement and balance sheet with all line items
\item {} ✅ Complete ALL three scenarios (Bull/Base/Bear) with different parameters
\item {} ❌ Do not create simplified/abbreviated versions
\item {} ❌ Do not skip any of the 6 essential tabs
\item {} ❌ Do not skip historical financials extraction if needed
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Verification before proceeding to Task 3}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \UncheckedBox Historical financials extracted (if needed) or provided
\item {} \UncheckedBox Excel file created and can be opened
\item {} \UncheckedBox Model has all 6 essential tabs (Revenue Model, Income Statement, Cash Flow, Balance Sheet, Scenarios, DCF Inputs)
\item {} \UncheckedBox Historical data (3-5 years) incorporated
\item {} \UncheckedBox Projections complete (5 years forward)
\item {} \UncheckedBox Scenarios complete (Bull/Base/Bear)
\end{itemize}

\item {} Projected income statements
\item {} Projected cash flows
\item {} Revenue and EBITDA forecasts
\item {} DCF inputs (unlevered FCF)
\end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

This task requires the financial model from Task 2. Starting without it will result in incomplete work.


\textbf{IF TASK 2 IS NOT COMPLETE}: Stop immediately and inform the user that Task 2 (Financial Modeling) must be completed first. Do not attempt to proceed or create placeholder valuations.

\item {} - [ ] Revenue projections
\item {} - [ ] EBITDA projections

\textbf{Output}: Valuation Analysis (4-6 pages + Excel tabs)

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]

\item {} Comparable companies (5-10 peers with statistical summary)
\item {} Precedent transactions (if applicable)
\item {} Valuation football field
\item {} \textbf{Price target}: \$XX.XX
\item {} \textbf{Recommendation}: BUY/HOLD/SELL
\item {} \textbf{Upside}: XX\%
\item {} Key catalysts (3-5)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Files}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]

\item {} Excel tabs added to \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model\_\allowbreak{}[Date].xlsx} (from Task 2)

\item {} Sensitivity analysis tab

\item {} Valuation summary tab
\end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{⚠ DO NOT TAKE SHORTCUTS:}

\item {} ✅ Include statistical summary in comps table (max/75th/median/25th/min)
\item {} ✅ Create complete sensitivity analysis tab with multiple WACC and terminal growth scenarios
\item {} ✅ Write full 4-6 pages of valuation analysis (not abbreviated)

\item {} ❌ Do not skip comparable company analysis
\item {} ❌ Do not create simplified DCF without sensitivity
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Verification before proceeding to Task 4}:

\item {} ✅ \textbf{Create ALL tables from Task 2/3} - Extract every financial table, don't skip any
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Use Task 1 content verbatim} - Copy/paste full Company 101 sections (6-8K words)
\item {} ✅ \textbf{Professional quality only} - This must be indistinguishable from JPMorgan/Goldman Sachs research
\end{itemize}

\textbf{NEVER:}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ❌ ``This section would include...'' - WRITE THE ACTUAL SECTION
\item {} ❌ ``Charts would be inserted here...'' - INSERT THE ACTUAL CHARTS
\item {} ❌ ``See financial model for details...'' - EXTRACT AND INCLUDE THE DETAILS
\item {} ❌ Skip sections due to length - Every section MUST be complete
\item {} ❌ Abbreviate for token conservation - Use whatever tokens are needed
\end{itemize}

\textbf{This is publication-ready institutional research. Spare no effort, tokens, or detail.}


\textbf{Output}: Comprehensive Equity Research Report (.docx)


\textbf{Specifications}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Length}: 30-50 pages (MINIMUM 30)
\item {} \textbf{Word count}: 10,000-15,000 words (MINIMUM 10,000)
\item {} \textbf{Charts}: 25-35 embedded images
\item {} \textbf{Tables}: 12-20 comprehensive tables
\item {} \textbf{Format}: Professional DOCX with clickable hyperlinks
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Structure}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Page 1: Investment Summary (INITIATING COVERAGE format)
\item {} Pages 2-5: Investment thesis \& risks
\item {} Pages 6-17: Company 101
\item {} Pages 18-30: Financial analysis \& projections
\item {} Pages 31-40: Valuation analysis
\item {} Pages 41-50: Appendices
\end{itemize}

\textbf{File name}: \emph{[Company]\_\allowbreak{}Initiation\_\allowbreak{}Report\_\allowbreak{}[Date].docx}


\textbf{⚠ DELIVER ONLY THIS 1 DOCX FILE. NO executive summaries, no ``highlights'' documents, no extra files.}


\textbf{Final Verification}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \UncheckedBox Report is 30-50 pages
\item {} \UncheckedBox Word count is 10,000-15,000
\item {} \UncheckedBox 25-35 charts embedded
\item {} \UncheckedBox 12-20 tables included
\item {} \UncheckedBox All citations are clickable hyperlinks
\item {} \UncheckedBox Numbers match financial model exactly
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Input Verification Protocol}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Why Input Verification Matters}\mbox{}\par

Each task has strict prerequisites. Running a task without required inputs will:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Waste time with incomplete work
\item {} Produce low-quality outputs
\item {} Require rework
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Always verify inputs before starting a task.}


\subparagraph{Verification Checklist by Task}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Task 1}: ✅ No verification needed (independent)


\textbf{Task 2}: Check historical financials available


\textbf{Task 3}: Check financial model exists and is accessible


\textbf{Task 4}: Check both model (Task 2) AND valuation (Task 3) exist


\textbf{Task 5}: Check ALL previous tasks complete (1, 2, 3, 4)


\subparagraph{What to Do if Verification Fails}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} IF prerequisites are missing:
\item {} 1. Stop the current task
\item {} 2. Identify which prerequisite task(s) need to be completed
\item {} 3. Execute the missing task(s) first
\item {} 4. Return to current task once prerequisites are met
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Example Verification Dialogue}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} User: ``Use initiating-coverage, Task 5 for Tesla''
\item {} Claude: ``Starting Task 5: Report Assembly for Tesla
\item {} VERIFICATION - Checking prerequisites:
\item {} - Task 1 (Company research)... ❌ NOT FOUND
\item {} - Task 2 (Financial model)... ❌ NOT FOUND
\item {} - Task 3 (Valuation)... ❌ NOT FOUND
\item {} - Task 4 (Charts)... ❌ NOT FOUND
\item {} ⚠ VERIFICATION FAILED
\item {} Task 5 requires ALL previous tasks to be complete.
\item {} To proceed:
\item {} 1. Complete Task 1: Company Research
\item {} 2. Complete Task 2: Financial Modeling
\item {} 3. Complete Task 3: Valuation Analysis
\item {} 4. Complete Task 4: Chart Generation
\item {} 5. Then return to Task 5
\item {} Would you like to start with Task 1 first?``
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Task Reference Files}\mbox{}\par

Detailed instructions for each task are in separate reference files to keep this skill lean:


\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{references/task1-company-research.md} - Company research workflow
\item {} \textbf{references/task2-financial-modeling.md} - Financial modeling workflow
\item {} \textbf{references/task3-valuation.md} - Valuation methodology
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Also see: references/valuation-methodologies.md for DCF/comps deep dive
\end{itemize}
\item {} \textbf{references/task4-chart-generation.md} - Chart generation workflow
\item {} \textbf{references/task5-report-assembly.md} - Report writing workflow
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Also see: assets/report-template.md for report structure
\item {} Also see: assets/quality-checklist.md for quality checks
\end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{When to load reference files}: Load ONLY the reference file associated with the specific task being performed. These files are very large - do not load multiple reference files at once. Read the appropriate task reference file at the start of the task for detailed step-by-step instructions.


\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Quality Standards}\mbox{}\par

All outputs meet institutional standards from leading investment banks (JPMorgan, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley):


\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Comprehensive}: Meet all minimum requirements
\item {} \textbf{Detailed}: Specific data and examples, not generic statements
\item {} \textbf{Quantified}: Lead with numbers and metrics
\item {} \textbf{Cited}: Proper sources with clickable hyperlinks
\item {} \textbf{Professional}: Institutional-quality formatting
\item {} \textbf{Accurate}: All numbers verified and cross-checked
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Important Notes}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Task Independence}\mbox{}\par

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Task 1} can run anytime (no dependencies)
\item {} \textbf{Task 2} can run anytime (just needs historical data)
\item {} \textbf{Tasks 1 \& 2} can run in parallel
\item {} \textbf{Task 3} requires Task 2
\item {} \textbf{Task 4} requires Tasks 2 \& 3
\item {} \textbf{Task 5} requires Tasks 1, 2, 3, \& 4
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Session Management}\mbox{}\par

\textbf{Same session}: Outputs automatically available to subsequent tasks


\textbf{Different sessions}: Reference previous task outputs explicitly

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ``Use Task 3 with the model from yesterday at [path]''
\item {} ``Use Task 5 with the research document at [path]''
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{File Organization}\mbox{}\par

Recommended structure during workflow:

\textbf{Structured Template}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} ProjectFolder/
\item {} Task1\_\allowbreak{}Research/
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Research\_\allowbreak{}Document.md
\item {} Task2\_\allowbreak{}Model/
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Financial\_\allowbreak{}Model.xlsx
\item {} Task3\_\allowbreak{}Valuation/
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Valuation\_\allowbreak{}Analysis.pdf
\item {} Task4\_\allowbreak{}Charts/
\item {} chart\_\allowbreak{}01.png
\item {} ... (25-35 files)
\item {} Task5\_\allowbreak{}Report/
\item {} [Company]\_\allowbreak{}Initiation\_\allowbreak{}Report.docx
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{No End-to-End Execution}\mbox{}\par

This skill does \textbf{NOT} support running all tasks automatically in sequence. Each task must be explicitly requested and verified.


\textbf{Why}: This ensures:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Quality control at each stage
\item {} Ability to review outputs before proceeding
\item {} Flexibility to pause/resume workflow
\item {} Clear verification of prerequisites
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.5em}\hrule\vspace{0.5em}

\subparagraph{Success Criteria}\mbox{}\par

A successful initiation report workflow should:

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Complete all 5 tasks in order
\item {} Pass all input verifications
\item {} Meet all quality standards
\item {} Produce all required deliverables
\item {} Numbers cross-check between outputs
\item {} Final report is publication-ready
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Output quality}: Institutional (JPMorgan/Goldman/Morgan Stanley level) \textbf{Use case}: First-time comprehensive coverage of a company


\vspace{0.8em}\hrule\vspace{0.8em}

\subsection{Skill: model-update}\label{file:skills-model-update-SKILL-md}

\paragraph{Model Update}\mbox{}\par

description: Update financial models with new data — quarterly earnings, management guidance, macro changes, or revised assumptions. Adjusts estimates, recalculates valuation, and flags material changes. Use after earnings, guidance updates, or when assumptions need refreshing. Triggers on ``update model'', ``plug earnings'', ``refresh estimates'', ``update numbers for [company]'', ``new guidance'', or ``revise estimates''.


\subparagraph{Workflow}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{Step 1: Identify What Changed}\mbox{}\par

Determine the update trigger:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} \textbf{Earnings release}: New quarterly actuals to plug in
\item {} \textbf{Guidance change}: Company updated forward outlook
\item {} \textbf{Estimate revision}: Analyst changing assumptions based on new data
\item {} \textbf{Macro update}: Interest rates, FX, commodity prices changed
\item {} \textbf{Event-driven}: M\&A, restructuring, new product, management change
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Step 2: Plug New Data}\mbox{}\par

\subparagraph{After Earnings}\mbox{}\par
Update the model with reported actuals:


\begingroup
\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
\begin{longtable}{>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.184\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.184\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.184\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.184\textwidth}>{\RaggedRight\arraybackslash\hspace{0pt}}p{0.184\textwidth}}
\toprule
{} \textbf{Line Item} & \textbf{Prior Estimate} & \textbf{Actual} & \textbf{Delta} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
{} Revenue &  &  &  &  \\
{} Gross Margin &  &  &  &  \\
{} Operating Expenses &  &  &  &  \\
{} EBITDA &  &  &  &  \\
{} EPS &  &  &  &  \\
{} [Key metric 1] &  &  &  &  \\
{} [Key metric 2] &  &  &  &  \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\textbf{Segment Detail} (if applicable):

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Update each segment's revenue and margin
\item {} Note any segment mix shifts
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Balance Sheet / Cash Flow Updates}:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt,topsep=2pt]
\item {} Cash and debt balances
\item {} Share count (buybacks, dilution)
\item {} Capex actual vs. estimate
\item {} Working capital changes
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Step 3: Revise Forward Estimates}\mbox{}\par

Based on the new data, adjust forward estimates:

\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{2pt}
